**Theorem C (Cabling conjecture for L-space knots).** Let K ⊂ S³ be an L-space knot. If S³_n(K) is reducible for some slope n, then K is a cable knot C_{p,q}(J) and n = pq is the cabling slope. Moreover J is again an L-space knot and p/q > 2g(J) − 1.

Abstract: "We prove that if K ⊂ S³ is an L-space knot, then every 3-manifold obtained by Dehn surgery on K has at most two prime summands. More precisely: either the surgered manifold is irreducible, or it is a connected sum L(p,q)#S³_{q/p}(J), where C_{p,q}(J) = K is the cabling decomposition forced by the theorem of Hedden–Levine (the cabling conjecture for L-space knots) and the second summand is irreducible by the classical non-integrality theorem of Culler–Gordon–Luecke–Shalen. The answer to the title question is therefore affirmative."

Introduction: context: cabling conjecture; L-space knots; statement Main Theorem 1.1; strategy paragraph; remark on hypotheses.

Some final wording for the introduction's statement of the main theorem:

Main Theorem. Let K ⊂ S³ be an L-space knot and let r ∈ ℚ∪{∞} be a surgery slope. Then the closed, oriented 3-manifold S³_r(K) has at most two prime summands. More precisely:
(i) If S³_r(K) is irreducible, it is prime, hence has one prime summand.
(ii) If S³_r(K) is reducible, then K is a cable C_{p,q}(J) with p ≥ 2, q ≠ 0, gcd(p,q)=1, r = pq, and
S³_r(K) ≅ L(p,q) # S³_{q/p}(J),
where L(p,q) = S³_{p/q}(O) is a lens space and the second summand S³_{q/p}(J) is irreducible; in particular S³_r(K) has exactly two prime summands.

Let me draft §2.1 conventions precisely:

"Let K ⊂ S³ be an oriented knot with exterior E_K = S³∖int N(K). Let μ_K ∈ H1(∂E_K) be a meridian and λ_K the Seifert longitude. For relatively prime integers p, q with q > 0, the slope p/q is the primitive class p[λ_K] + q[μ_K] ∈ H1(∂E_K); the p/q-Dehn surgery is S³_{p/q}(K) := E_K ∪ S¹×D², glued so that the meridian of S¹×D² is identified with a curve of slope p/q. We write S³_n(K) := S³_{n/1}(K) for n ∈ ℤ. A slope is integral if q = 1 and non-integral otherwise. It is standard that H1(S³_{p/q}(K)) ≅ ℤ_{|p|} for p ≠ 0 [proof included]."

Then cables:

"Let J ⊂ S³ be oriented with meridian μ_J and Seifert longitude λ_J on T = ∂N(J). For p ≥ 2 and q ∈ ℤ∖{0} with gcd(p,|q|) = 1, the (p,q)-cable of J is the oriented knot C_{p,q}(J) ⊂ T representing p[λ_J] + q[μ_J] ∈ H1(T). ... winding number p; lk(C, J) = q; C_{p,q}(O) = T(p,q). The cabling slope is pq (with respect to the Seifert framing of C_{p,q}(J))."
